- `tblastx` holds back the two-hit extensions of a unit in batches of 16 and prefetches the query and subject lines each one reads first, for subjects whose unit query-group span has at least 256 Ki letters (for one query, about 0.8 Mb or more), on x86-64: Sakai x MG1655 at 8 threads x0.940 (`LOSAT_X_TBXEXTBATCH`, one of the switches above; `=0` turns it off; same results; runs with `LOSAT_TIMING` or `LOSAT_X_STATS` do not use it).
- `tblastx` asks the CPU to fetch the diagonal cell of the lookup hit 16 entries ahead while it scans a subject whose diagonal table of a query group has at least 1.5 MiB (`LOSAT_X_TBXPREFETCH`, one of the switches above; a value switch: the smallest table in KiB, built-in 1,536; x86-64 only); a prefetch changes no value, so reports do not change. It acts on the unit path of `LOSAT_X_PAIRPAR`; `LOSAT_X_TBXPREFETCH=0` turns it off. On the workstation Sakai against MG1655 takes 0.86 of the time at 8 threads, and pairs with 2-5 MiB tables 0.97-0.98 at 8 threads and 0.96-1.00 at one thread ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- The Node threaded runner (`LOSAT/tests/wasi_thread_host.js`) boots a command's thread workers before `_start` by default (`LOSAT_WASI_PRESPAWN` unset is now `auto`; `=0` boots none) and keeps a reactor's thread workers between searches (`LOSAT_WASI_RECYCLE`, `1` when unset, `0` turns it off): a returned thread's worker instantiates the module again and waits for the next thread, as the browser's thread host does. TBLASTX at 8 threads on the i9: a p03 command 0.83-1.12 s instead of 0.89-1.31 s; in a reactor, every search after the first spent 1 ms instead of 150-282 ms in the host's spawns (p03 0.39-0.46 s instead of 0.57-0.76 s). Reports do not change.
- `tblastx` reads the scores of its two-hit ungapped extension from one byte table indexed by both residue codes, without the range tests and row multiply of each step (`LOSAT_X_TBXUNGAP`, one of the switches above; `=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_TBXUNGAPSHADOW`); the walks keep NCBI's statements, order and exit tests, so reports do not change. On the workstation (8 rounds, against the build before it) MelaMJNV against PemoMJNVA at one thread takes 0.978 of the time, AP027131 against AP027133 (genetic code 4) 0.971 at one thread and 0.984 at 8, Sakai against the first 1 Mb of MG1655 0.988 at 8 threads ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `tblastn` in the multithreaded WebAssembly build (`wasm32-wasip1-threads`) starts no thread beyond the `-num_threads` search pool: it packs the `-subject` records on the thread that reads them, as at one thread, where the native build packs them on a second thread while the next ones are read. Before, at two or more threads it started one host worker more than asked for. Reports do not change.
- `tblastx` with two or more threads masks the frames of a query batch with SEG on the search pool, one task per frame, instead of one frame after the other before the lookup table is built (`LOSAT_X_TBXQFRAMES`, one of the switches above since 2026-10-18; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; `LOSAT_X_TBXQFRAMESSHADOW=1` also masks them serially and compares); a frame's mask depends on its letters and the SEG parameters only, so reports do not change. On an i9 at 8 threads the SEG step took 0.223 -> 0.044 s for AP027131 x AP027133 (wall 1.86 -> 1.72 s) and 0.048 -> 0.009 s for MelaMJNV x PemoMJNVA (0.247 -> 0.210 s); max RSS within 2 MiB ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- TBLASTX at two or more threads returns the large blocks of its parallel seed stage to the system when it frees them, on Linux with glibc where transparent huge pages are not "always" (`LOSAT_X_SEEDUNMAP`, now one of the switches above, so reports do not change): for a unit whose query span and subject chunk both have at least 65,536 letters, the diagonal table and record vector that each split of a subject's units keeps, and a copy of a unit's records of at least 128 KiB that lives until the merge, get a capacity above glibc's largest dynamic mmap threshold, so glibc maps them and unmaps them when they are dropped instead of keeping them in the worker threads' arenas through the linking call, where the search peaks. Where one side is short (many short queries or subjects) the arenas keep handing the same blocks back, since mapping them anew at every split cost x1.05-1.07 of the time; with THP "always" (WSL2, the RHEL family's default) the kernel would back the mapped blocks with 2 MiB pages and raise the memory, so nothing changes there; and at one thread, where glibc's main arena is trimmed and nothing is retained, nothing changes. On the workstation (8 rounds, every report the frozen one) max RSS at 8 threads went from 128 to 118 MiB on AP027131 against AP027133 (genetic code 4), from 176 to 163 MiB on NZ_CP006932 against itself and from 41 to 37 MiB on MelaMJNV against PemoMJNVA, with the time x1.000-1.006. `LOSAT_X_SEEDUNMAP=0` turns it off.
- `tblastn` reads a packed subject's six frames (every `-db` sequence, and `-subject` without `-subject_loc`) from the packed bytes, eight codons from one word, and with AVX2 32 codons at a time (`LOSAT_X_TBNXLATESIMD`, one of the switches above); the frames, and so the output, do not change. One protein against the human genome (`-db`): 0.88 of the time at one thread (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 takes 1.9 times as long) and 0.94 at 8 threads; the other measured cases 0.99-1.00 ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastx -db` checks the residues of each database sequence (no gap character, no byte past NCBIstdaa) in one pass without an early exit, which the compiler vectorizes; the old check stopped at the first bad byte and read one byte at a time. The same sequences are refused with the same messages. One 300-nt query against the first UniRef50 volume (3.0 G residues, one thread, workstation) takes 0.955-0.96 of the time. `blastp -db` checks its blocks in its own pass and is unchanged.
- `tblastx` holds each ungapped HSP of its linking list in 88 bytes instead of 152 on 64-bit targets: the record keeps its offsets, sequence lengths, query context, identity count and list positions in 32-bit fields (NCBI BLAST+ keeps them in `Int4`) and the subject frame index in one byte, set only with checked conversions and read back in the types used before, so reports do not change. No switch: a record layout is not chosen at run time. On the workstation (8 cores, 8 rounds) max RSS falls from 158 to 137 MiB on AP027131 against AP027133 (code 4) at 8 threads and from 98 to 92 at 1, and from 217 to 184 MiB (129 to 107 at 1 thread) on NZ_CP006932 against itself; the search takes 0.96-0.99 of the time (merging the seed stage's lists 0.64-0.80, the first linking call's sorts 0.74-0.87).
- `tblastn` links a subject's HSPs testing only the candidates whose subject positions can make an admissible set, in NCBI's order and with NCBI's tests, instead of every HSP in the query-offset window (`LOSAT_X_TBNLINKWIN`, one of the switches above; shadow `LOSAT_X_TBNLINKWINSHADOW`, opt-in). The links and E-values, and so the output, do not change. One protein against the zebrafish genome (`-db`, one thread): 0.60 of the time (24.8 -> 14.8 s; NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 31.3 s); against the human genome 0.87; the fly genome x one batch of proteins at 8 threads 0.76. With the other two switches on: 0.58, 0.82 (0.84 at 8 threads) and 0.81 ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastx` finds the subject words that have lookup hits without a branch per word (the loop of `tblastn`'s `LOSAT_X_TBNSCANBF`: the words whose presence bit is set are listed first, then their hits are taken in position order), in its default seed loop and in the fused loop of `LOSAT_X_BXDIAG` (`LOSAT_X_BXSCANBF`, a switch of the strict set; `LOSAT_X_BXSCANBF=0` turns it off). The output does not change. On the workstation (one thread, `-db`, against NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0): a 300-nt fly cDNA piece against the first UniRef50 volume (3.0 G residues; 34 % of the words have hits) takes 0.60 of the time without it and 0.66 of NCBI's time, one 2.1-kb cDNA (84 %) 0.89 and 0.85, and 800 cDNAs against the yeast proteome (98 %) 0.99-1.00.
- `blastx` scans the subject and applies the two-hit diagonal test in one loop and tests the hits of one lookup cell in batches of eight in AVX2 registers (the loop of `LOSAT_X_BPSEED` with the cell test of `LOSAT_X_BPDIAG`), where the lookup table is the standard one (`-word_size 3`, the default), on an x86-64 CPU with AVX2 and for the query batches whose lookup table has at least 5 hits per used cell (`LOSAT_X_BXDIAG`, a switch of the strict set, with `LOSAT_X_BXDIAGMIN` in hundredths: 500; `LOSAT_X_BXDIAG=0` turns it off, and set to a value it runs on any CPU and with `per-hit` or `scalar` cell tests; shadow `LOSAT_X_BXDIAGSHADOW`). A batch with fewer hits per cell is scanned by the direct loop, which is faster there. The output does not change. On the workstation (one thread, `-db`, with `LOSAT_X_BXSCANBF`, against `LOSAT_X_BXDIAG=0`): 800 fly cDNAs against the yeast proteome take 0.71 of the time (about 0.61 of NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db`), one 2.1-kb cDNA against the first UniRef50 volume 0.93, and a 300-nt piece of it the same time (no batch fused; fusing it took 1.20 times as long).
- `blastx` scans a subject shorter than a word less one letter (one residue with the default word size) from a copy with sentinels after it: with a two-hit window its one scan position reads a word from position 0, which ran past the sentinel (NCBI BLAST+ reads the bytes that follow in its buffer). One scan position extends no hit, so reports do not change.
- TBLASTX builds the full lookup table of a query batch, the table of the reference seed loop, in place from per-cell counts, as BLASTX and BLASTP do by default, instead of copying it out of one list per cell (`LOSAT_X_TBXLUT`, one of the switches above since 2026-10-18, with `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARGATE`; shadow `LOSAT_X_TBXLUTSHADOW`), so reports do not change. With `-threshold +inf` and the reference loop (`LOSAT_X_PAIRPAR=0`) the option-sweep input takes 3.31 GB instead of 14.2 GB (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 5.86 GB); on the batches the gate below sends to the reference loop it takes the same time ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `tblastn` with two or more threads searches the query batches the rule leaves one after another (for example all of them when there are fewer batches than threads) at the same time, each on a pool of its own with its share of the threads, when there are fewer than two subjects per thread (`LOSAT_X_TBNOWNPOOLS`, one of the switches above, in the set with `LOSAT_X_TBNBATCH` and `LOSAT_X_TBNBATCHRULE`; native builds only, since in WebAssembly only the main thread can start threads); reports do not change. Before, they were searched one after another with all threads. One 4.7 Mb genome with 16 query batches at 32 threads: 0.20 of the time ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `tblastn` with two or more threads searches query batches side by side only for the full waves (a multiple of the threads) and only when the subjects hold at most 24 Mb or there are fewer than two subjects per thread (`LOSAT_X_TBNBATCHRULE`, one of the switches above, in the set with `LOSAT_X_TBNBATCH`): the other batches are searched one after another with all threads, as NCBI searches them, so reports do not change. Before, every batch went side by side once there was one per thread. On 28,036 16S subjects (40.6 Mb) with 10 query batches at 8 threads the search takes 0.68 of the time of `LOSAT_X_TBNBATCH` alone, on one 0.6 Mb genome 0.77 ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastx` with two or more threads searches the subjects (`-db` or `-subject`) of a query batch it searches as one chunk as a flow of tasks of 64 consecutive subjects that the other threads search ahead, handed to the hit lists in subject order, instead of waves of 64 subjects per thread that wait for their slowest thread (`LOSAT_X_BXSUBJFLOW`, in the strict set from the pull request that added it, 2026-10-17; `=0` returns to the waves; shadow `LOSAT_X_BXSUBJFLOWSHADOW`); reports do not change. Ten transcripts (10.9 kb) against Swiss-Prot `-db`: 16 threads 5.23 to 2.30 s (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db` 2.58 s), 8 threads 7.11 to 3.89 s (NCBI 4.12 s; medians of 3 rounds). With `LOSAT_TIMING`, `blastx_subject_waves` and `blastx_subject_flow` lines ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastx` with two or more threads searches its short query batches as a flow of tasks instead of waves of four batches per thread, writing the reports in input order as they finish, and searches the input's last batches side by side with the whole pool (`LOSAT_X_BXFLOW`, a switch of the strict set, natively; `LOSAT_X_BXFLOW=0` turns it off; the Web reactors keep the waves). The output does not change. Against the yeast proteome at 16 threads it takes 0.71-0.84 of the time of the waves on 800 fly cDNAs and 0.56-0.58 on 50 cDNAs, and the same time on one batch. Timed again as a default (8 threads, with the other blastx defaults): 800 cDNAs take 0.82-0.84 of the time with `LOSAT_X_BXFLOW=0` (0.57-0.62 of NCBI's), 50 cDNAs 0.73-0.74, one batch 1.00; the largest memory is about 5 % higher on 800 cDNAs (70 MB against 67 MB; NCBI 125 MB).
- `tblastn` scans the subject's words against the lookup table without a branch per word (the words whose presence bit is set are listed first, then their hits are copied in order, with NCBI's stop and resume when the offset array is full). The offset pairs, and so the output, do not change (`LOSAT_X_TBNSCANBF`, one of the switches above). One 411-residue protein against the human genome (`-db`): 0.73 of the time at one thread (45.6 -> 33.2 s; NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 44.9 s) and 0.77 at 8 threads; one protein against the zebrafish genome 0.79; batches of many proteins 0.98-0.99 ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastn` (all tasks) holds less memory while it searches, with the same output: the query once (one BLASTNA buffer with sentinels, as NCBI BLAST+ holds it, instead of about seven copies, with the four-base bytes of the ungapped extension computed where they are read), the final HSP lists at their exact size, and no growth slack in FASTA records of 1 MiB or more (all programs). Max RSS, same-run measurements: megablast of Sakai (5.5 Mb) against MG1655 at 8 threads 197 to 146 MiB (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db` 153 MiB); 100 16S queries against 28,036 16S sequences at one thread 135.5 to 109.1 MiB.
- `blastn` (all tasks) at one thread hands each subject's HSPs to the hit lists as soon as the subject is searched, as NCBI BLAST+ updates its hit lists one subject at a time, instead of holding the HSPs of every subject of a query batch until all are searched. Reports do not change. Megablast of 100 16S queries against 28,036 16S sequences (40.6 Mb) at one thread: peak memory 237 to 175 MiB (same-run measurement). With two or more threads the same is on by default (`LOSAT_X_BNSTREAM`, one of the switches of the strict set below; `LOSAT_X_BNSTREAM=0` turns it off): the threads search at most 64 subjects per thread ahead of the subject whose HSPs go to the hit lists next, while one of them hands the HSPs over in subject order; on the same search on a 2-socket Xeon workstation, 202 to 141 MiB at 0.94 times the time at 8 threads and 211 to 145 MiB at 0.88 times at 16 threads ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- The exact performance paths of the 2026-10 performance work (the `LOSAT_X_*` switches of the "strict set") are on by default in every program, natively and in WebAssembly. Each of them gives the reference result by construction, so reports do not change. `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` runs the previous paths, and `LOSAT_X_<NAME>=0` turns one switch off ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md), "Performance switches"). The Web reactors receive no environment and always run the default.
- Costs of the default: megablast keeps one byte per base of a subject while it is searched, and tblastn keeps up to 256 MiB of subject translations for a search of four or more query batches (20,000 query residues per batch); a search of up to three batches keeps none. TBLASTX builds only the lookup tables its seed stage scans, frees them before the HSP list of the last subject of a query batch is merged and linked, and sorts and links the HSP list of a subject without a second copy; on a 0.66 Mb by 0.61 Mb pair its peak memory is 163 MiB at 8 threads and 111 MiB at 1 thread, below the previous paths (320 and 249 MiB in the same measurement). On targets other than x86_64, aarch64 and WebAssembly no vector X-drop kernel exists, and the scalar code runs there.
- TBLASTX gives each thread of a search the next subject when it is free (`LOSAT_X_TBXNEXT`) and makes a subject's frames only while the search reads them (`LOSAT_X_TBXFRAMES`); both are now among the switches above, so reports do not change. Before, rayon cut the subjects into pieces before the search, and the long subjects a genome lists first (its chromosome arms) were searched one after another on one thread. On the workstation (medians of alternating pairs, every report equal to NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db -num_threads 1`) 3 fruit fly transcripts against the fruit fly genome database (1,870 sequences) took 9.75 s at one thread (11.59 s before; NCBI 10.05 s) with a peak memory of 107 MiB (257 MiB; NCBI 162 MiB), and 2.58 s at 8 threads (11.48 s; NCBI 2.73 s) with 316 MiB (257 MiB; NCBI 359 MiB). `LOSAT_X_TBXNEXT=0` and `LOSAT_X_TBXFRAMES=0` turn them off.
- TBLASTX holds less memory in its seed stage (`LOSAT_X_SEEDCOMPACT`, now one of the switches above, so reports do not change): a unit of the parallel seed stage keeps its saved ungapped hits as 28-byte records, the shape NCBI BLAST+ keeps them in, copied out at their exact length, and the merge makes each chunk's list at its exact length and moves the first one into the frame's list instead of copying it. On the workstation (8 rounds, every report equal to NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0), max RSS went from 109 to 87 MiB at one thread and from 157 to 148 MiB at 8 threads on the 0.66 Mb by 0.61 Mb pair (AP027131 x AP027133, genetic code 4), and from 130 to 121 MiB and from 215 to 209 MiB on a genome against itself (NZ_CP006932); the whole search took x0.97-0.99 of the time. `LOSAT_X_SEEDCOMPACT=0` turns it off.
- TBLASTX merges the HSP lists of a subject's parallel seed-stage units without sorting the whole list again at each step: each unit sorts its own list, a chunk's lists are merged in order, the conversion sorts only runs of HSPs that tie on score and subject range, and each frame's list is merged into the subject's (`LOSAT_X_TBXKMERGE`, one of the switches above, so reports do not change). Measured at 8 and 16 threads, the whole search is 1-4 % faster (three ~650 kb genomes against three: 11.81 -> 11.68 s at 16 threads, 17.29 -> 16.86 s at 8); the serial merge step after the units shrank from 2.30 to 0.92 s there and from 5.82 to 1.66 s for Sakai x MG1655, with the units' own sorts now done in parallel inside the units.
- `tblastn` with two or more threads on many subjects hands each thread a chunk of consecutive subjects at a time instead of one subject, cut by residues as NCBI's database reader cuts them for its search threads: at most `min(2^30, F) / (4 * threads) + 1` bases, at least one subject, where F is a quarter of the subjects' bases (the `.nsq` file `makeblastdb` would write), so a long subject is a chunk of its own (`LOSAT_X_TBNOIDCHUNK`, one of the switches above). On 28,036 16S subjects (40.6 Mb) at 8 threads a 55-protein search took a median of 1.35 s against 2.81 s without it, and a one-protein search 0.61 s against 1.50 s; 20 zebrafish proteins against the fly genome (1,870 records, the chromosome arms first) took 7.0 s against 6.8 s (four rounds on a loaded machine). Reports and peak memory are those of the search without it.
- Megablast's preliminary greedy alignment runs the fused loop by default (`LOSAT_X_GREEDYFUSE`, one of the switches above). Measured in #171 on 100 16S queries against RefSeq 16S (28,036 subjects), one thread, on the workstation: wall time 0.74-0.81 of the reference; NCBI 2.17.0 `-db` over LOSAT 1.24 -> 1.63; the same bytes.
- `tblastx` with two or more threads sorts the index orders of the HSP list of a subject faster (`LOSAT_X_LINKSORT`, one of the switches above): the linking call sorts its output order once where it sorted twice, and a long index sort runs as a stable parallel sort on the search pool, with the same order as before. At 8 threads the whole search took x0.926 of the time on the 0.66 Mb by 0.61 Mb pair (AP027131 x AP027133, genetic code 4) and x0.956 on a genome against itself (NZ_CP006932); at 1 thread the difference is within the noise. Reports and peak memory are unchanged.
- On aarch64 (Linux arm64, macOS on Apple silicon) the X-drop gapped alignment of `blastn -task blastn` and of the protein programs runs in 128-bit NEON vectors by default (`LOSAT_X_DPNEON`, one of the switches above), with the kernel bodies x86_64 and WebAssembly run; before, aarch64 ran the scalar code. On the GitHub Linux arm64 runner (ubuntu-24.04-arm), one thread, median of 7 rounds: `blastn -task blastn` AP027202 x LC738875 10.38 s -> 3.50 s and AP027152 x AP027202 0.201 s -> 0.105 s, `blastp` WSSV x PajaWSV 2.04 s -> 1.25 s, `tblastn` 0.81 s -> 0.67 s, `blastx` 0.86 s -> 0.70 s; megablast does not use this alignment and is unchanged. Reports are the same bytes.
- On aarch64, `blastp` and `tblastn` solve the Newton problems of the composition adjustment eight per round instead of four (`LOSAT_X_NEWTONLANES8`, one of the switches above); each problem keeps its operations, so reports do not change. Measured on the GitHub runners with bench5's blastp_wssv / tblastn_small at one thread: wall time 0.98 / 0.96 of four lanes on Linux arm64 (Neoverse-N2) and 0.97 / 0.89 on macOS arm64. x86-64 and WebAssembly keep four lanes.
- `tblastn` does less work per subject (`LOSAT_X_TBNSTATE` and `LOSAT_X_TBNXLATE`, two of the switches above): a search thread keeps the scan state of its word finder and gapped alignment from one subject to the next, as NCBI's search threads do, instead of making it for each subject and clearing its diagonal table for each piece of a subject (it keeps one state, about 1 MB for 84,000 query residues, until it ends or its next tblastn search), and the six frames of a subject are read from codon tables, as NCBI translates the compressed subject. Reports do not change. On the workstation at 8 threads (alternating rounds; the same reports; peak memory equal or lower): 55 proteins against 28,036 16S sequences (40.6 Mb) 1.07 -> 0.85 s (x0.79; at one thread x0.70), zebrafish proteins against the fly genome 15.8 -> 13.7 s (x0.86), and on a build before #191 fly proteins against the zebrafish transcriptome x0.76, against the human transcriptome x0.74 and against the zebrafish genome x0.87. The 16S search then takes 0.84-0.96 of the time of NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 with `-db` at 1, 8, 16 and 32 threads.
- `tblastn` with two or more threads on many subjects no longer waits between entries of chunks and searches the pieces of a long subject on all threads (`LOSAT_X_TBNOIDNEXT` and `LOSAT_X_TBNOIDUNITS`, two of the switches above): a thread takes the next chunk of subjects when it is free, as NCBI's search threads ask the database for the next chunk, and the (frame, piece) units of a subject longer than a chunk's budget (a chromosome) are searched by the threads that have no chunk left, with the results entered in NCBI's order. Reports do not change. On the workstation (alternating rounds; the same reports and CPU time): zebrafish proteins against the fly genome at 8 threads 13.7 -> 9.7 s (x0.71), fly proteins against the zebrafish genome x0.89, against the human genome at 16 threads 272.6 -> 265.4 s (x0.97), against 28,036 16S sequences and the zebrafish transcriptome x1.00 and x1.01.
- `tblastx` links the groups of a subject's HSP list where they lie in the list (`LOSAT_X_LINKSLICES`, one of the switches above): the linking call no longer cuts the sorted list into one list per group and copies the linked groups into a new list, so the list does not exist twice. At 8 threads the whole search took x0.900 of the time on the 0.66 Mb by 0.61 Mb pair (AP027131 x AP027133, genetic code 4), x0.948 on AP027131 against 200 contigs and x0.988 on a genome against itself (NZ_CP006932); at 1 thread the difference is within the noise. Max RSS on NZ_CP006932 against itself went from 141 to 130 MiB at 1 thread and from 235 to 215 MiB at 8 threads; on the other two it is unchanged (on the pair the search peaks in its seed stage). Reports are unchanged.
- `tblastn -subject` holds its subjects packed, about a quarter of a byte per base, instead of their letters (`LOSAT_X_TBNSTORE`, one of the switches above): each record is packed as soon as it is read (with two or more threads by a second thread while the reader goes on), the frames of a subject are translated from the packed bases, as NCBI BLAST+ translates the compressed subject, and the composition-based redo and the report make only the windows of a subject they align. Reports do not change. On the workstation at 8 threads (alternating rounds, the same reports) peak memory fell from 2,556-2,627 to 1,261-1,263 MiB for one fly protein against the zebrafish genome (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 with `-db`: about 1,290 MiB), from 2,719-2,831 to 1,344-1,391 MiB for 61 proteins against it (NCBI `-db` 1,440) and from 6,568 to 3,595-3,602 MiB against the human genome (NCBI `-db` 4,096); against the human transcriptome 1,863 to 975-979 MiB (NCBI `-db` 418, which reads the definition line of a hit only). The time was 0.94-1.00 of the search without it at 8 threads and 0.98-0.99 at one thread.
- `blastn` keeps one byte per subject base less during a search with many subjects: the traceback makes the uncompressed (blastna) letters of the subject it aligns from its record, as NCBI BLAST+ fetches them per subject in its traceback, instead of keeping them for every subject from the start. Reports do not change. Megablast of 100 16S queries against 28,036 16S sequences (40.6 Mb): peak memory 236 to 197 MiB at one thread and 246 to 206 MiB at 8 threads; with one query 119 to 79 MiB (same-run measurements). A search against one long subject keeps its peak, since its traceback encodes that subject.
- `blastn` keeps less memory during a search with many subjects: the name of each subject in LOSAT's debug and trace lines (`LOSAT_DEBUG_BLASTN`, the BLASTN trace) is made only while they are on, instead of being kept three times, once for the run and twice for each query batch. Reports do not change. Megablast against 28,036 16S sequences (40.6 Mb), same-run measurements: one query at one thread 68.1 to 64.2 MiB, 100 queries 135.3 to 131.0 MiB at one thread and 205.8 to 201.5 MiB at 8 threads.
- `blastn` prepares its subjects faster: it packs each subject's letters in one pass, resolving only the ambiguous letters (as before, from NCBI's `CRandom` seeded with the subject length, in the same order), instead of converting and resolving every letter into a second buffer first. The packed letters, and so the reports, do not change. At one thread a megablast search without hits against 28,036 16S sequences took a median of 0.17 s instead of 0.44 s, and a search with one query a median 0.72 of its previous time (8 alternating pairs each).
- `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISC=1` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off): `blastn -task dc-megablast` searches its two-hit seed stage on the search pool when one subject is searched with two or more threads and the query block is longer than 8,000 letters: the pool threads scan strips of the subject and the owner thread folds the word hits into the diagonal hash in the reference order, so reports do not change (`LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISCSHADOW=1` compares each chunk with the reference). The owner's fold limits the gain beyond 4 threads: on 5,000 D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, local pairs at load 22-28, wall time with the switch on was 0.49 of off at 4 threads and 0.56 at 8; peak memory +5 % ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17 (its helpers only yield on wasm32, where `std::thread::sleep` panics under the browser's WASI shim): dc-megablast of D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb -62..-74 % wall at 4-16 threads on the workstation; max RSS +10-72 MiB, below NCBI `-db`.
- `blastp -subject` with two or more threads searches its subjects in runs of consecutive subjects (`LOSAT_X_BPSUBJRUN`, one of the switches above; shadow `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJRUNSHADOW`, opt-in): each run clears its diagonal table once, instead of clearing it for every subject and making a new search scratch for every piece of work, so reports do not change. On the workstation (16 physical cores; medians of alternating pairs; every output hash equal; peak memory equal) `blastp-fast` of the human proteome against the yeast proteome with `-subject` took 0.89 of the time at 16 threads (6 pairs; 0.88 with `LOSAT_X_BPQBLOCK=20000`), 0.90-0.92 at 8 and 0.92 at 4; exact `blastp` 0.998 at 16 and 0.995 at 8; one thread is unchanged. `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJRUN=0` turns it off ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- Added the switch `LOSAT_X_BPDBCUT` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_BPDBCUTSHADOW`): with two or more threads, `blastp -db` (every BLASTP task) reads a database smaller than 4 MiB per thread in blocks of about a quarter of its sequence bytes per thread (at least 4 KiB) instead of 1 MiB, so that every thread has blocks to search; larger databases keep blocks of 1 MiB, and one thread is unchanged. Each block still starts at the one-thread order's diagonal offset and the results are merged in ordinal order, so reports and errors do not change. On the workstation at 16 threads against the yeast proteome database (2.92 MB; alternating runs, the same outputs): blastp-fast of 23,879 human proteins 19.9 -> 8.7 s (x0.44; NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db` at 16 threads took 15.6 s in an earlier window), exact blastp of 3,000 human proteins 29.5 -> 9.3 s (x0.31), and the OrthoFinder jobs of the Arabidopsis and C. elegans proteomes x0.31 and x0.32. Peak memory is unchanged for blastp-fast and grows by one search scratch per thread that now has work for the exact search (37 -> 43-44 MiB) ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: on the workstation at 16 threads -53 % wall for blastp-fast and -70 % for exact blastp against a 2.9 MB OrthoFinder species database, the yeast proteome all against all 32.7-35.1 s against 99.2-99.3 s (NCBI `-db` 60.5-61.6 s); max RSS within 9 MiB, below NCBI's.
- Added the switch `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJMETA` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJMETASHADOW`): the `blastp -subject` search in query blocks (`LOSAT_X_BPQBLOCK`) makes the subjects' tabular IDs and titles once for all blocks instead of once per block, on the block's thread while the other threads waited; they depend on each subject record only, so reports and errors do not change. Human x yeast blastp-fast at 16 threads on the workstation (other blastp switches, `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJRUN` included, on in both runs): wall time 0.952 of the reference with blocks of 50,000 letters, 0.927 with blocks of 20,000 ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: blastp-fast human x yeast `-subject` at 16 threads -5 % wall on the workstation, max RSS unchanged.
- `LOSAT_X_DIAGLV=1` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_DIAGLVSHADOW`): `blastn` (all tasks, `-subject` and `-db`) with a query block longer than 8,000 letters keeps, per bucket of its diagonal hash, the highest level written since the table was made or cleared, and a seed's lookup of its own diagonal skips the walk of a bucket whose cells all lie below the level that could change the seed's decisions (the test of the explored area and, with two hits, the window test). The inserts still walk the chains, so the table and the reports do not change. On 1,775 D. melanogaster cDNAs (5.0 Mb, one query batch) against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L with `-task dc-megablast` (and `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISC`), on a 2-socket Xeon workstation, wall time with the switch on was 0.808 of off at 1 thread and 0.624 at 16 threads, and 0.600 at 16 threads with 3,492 cDNAs (two batches); `-task blastn` with the same query, where a lookup walks about two cells, 1.003 at 1 thread (0.3 % slower; instructions +0.1 %) and 0.997 at 16; peak memory unchanged ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: dc-megablast of D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb -8..-12 % wall at 16 threads (-27 % at 8) on the workstation, max RSS unchanged.
- `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARFOLD=1` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off): where the strips paths of `LOSAT_X_PAIRPAR` (`blastn` and `megablast`) and `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISC` (`dc-megablast`) run (one subject searched with two or more threads, query block longer than 8,000 letters), the word hits are folded into the diagonal hash by every thread of the pool instead of by the owner thread alone: the hash's 512 buckets are split into one partition per thread, and the threads fold the partitions (each partition's hits in scan order into a table of its own); the hit list and the table's cell numbers are then put back in the order of the one-thread fold, so reports do not change (the strips shadows `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARSHADOW` and `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISCSHADOW` compare every chunk with the switch). On 1,775 D. melanogaster cDNAs (5.0 Mb, one query batch) against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L with `-task dc-megablast` (with `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISC` and `LOSAT_X_DIAGLV` in both arms), on a 2-socket Xeon workstation, wall time with the switch on was 0.58 of off at 16 threads (1.37 -> 0.80 s), 0.69 at 8 and 0.83 at 4; with 3,492 cDNAs (two batches) 0.58 at 16 threads, and 0.96 s instead of 1.62 s with `LOSAT_X_QBATCHPAR`; `-task blastn` with the first query set 0.51 at 16 threads (2.83 -> 1.44 s). Peak memory +12-17 MiB for dc-megablast (90-107 -> 104-123 MiB) and 16-23 MiB less for blastn (137-151 -> 121-129 MiB) ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: dc-megablast of D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb -42..-49 % wall at 16 threads, plain blastn -12 % on the workstation; max RSS +7..+48 MiB (dc-megablast of 31,168 cDNAs +39 MiB, plain blastn of 1,775 cDNAs +48 MiB at 16 threads beside the earlier budget's QBATCHPAR slots), below NCBI `-db` in every cell.
- TBLASTX runs the unit path of `LOSAT_X_PAIRPAR` only for a query batch where it is expected to be faster than the reference seed loop (a translated query batch of more than about 65 kb, two or more threads with one subject holding most of the bases, or a `-threshold` below the default 13; `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARGATE`, one of the switches above since 2026-10-18, with `LOSAT_X_TBXLUT`), so reports do not change. On the workstation 10 fruit fly transcripts against the zebrafish cDNA database (57,775 sequences) take 46.7 s at one thread instead of 60.5 s (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db` 45.2 s), 6.09 s at 8 threads instead of 7.94 s (NCBI 5.90 s) and 3.24 s at 16 threads instead of 4.43 s (NCBI 3.27 s); longer queries keep the unit path and their time ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastp`'s two-hit seed stage calls the candidates of a lookup cell's AVX2 batches after the cell's last batch of eight by default, instead of at the end of each batch (`LOSAT_X_BPDEFER`, one of the switches above; it acts only where the AVX2 batches of `LOSAT_X_BPDIAG` run), so the batch loop has no branch on the candidates; the calls keep their order, so reports do not change. In a third run of the same 49 OrthoFinder-style jobs with it on as well, every output equalled the run without it, the max RSS stayed below NCBI's on every job, and the sum of the job times was 0.407 of NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0's (0.457 without it; NCBI's times from the same earlier run); on the same jobs (every 10th query protein, one thread each) the summed CPU cycles were 0.938 of the run without it ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `tblastx` scans the subject's words against a lookup table without a branch per word (`LOSAT_X_TBXSCANBF`, one of the switches above; tblastn's loop of `LOSAT_X_TBNSCANBF` applied to each subject range with NCBI's stops for room), for tables with presence bits in fewer than half of their cells (queries up to about 60 kb); other tables keep NCBI's loop, and reports do not change. On one thread the time is 0.72-0.73 for 2-5 kb queries against a 294 kb genome, 0.84 for 3 transcripts (2 kb) against the fruit fly genome database and 0.85 for 10 transcripts (18.8 kb) against the zebrafish cDNA database; the same for queries of 80 kb and more ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastp` finds the subject words with lookup hits without a branch per word (`LOSAT_X_BPSCANBF`, one of the switches above: the branch-free cell mode of `LOSAT_X_BXSCANBF` in blastp's two-hit seed stage); the cells and their order are the same, so reports do not change. One protein against a 3.0 G-residue UniRef50 volume takes 0.64-0.75 of the time at one thread (a 357-residue protein: 52.4 s, NCBI BLAST+ 71.7 s) and 0.76 at 8; searches with many query proteins take the same time ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- Added the switch `LOSAT_X_BPREDOPAR` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_BPREDOPARSHADOW`): with two or more threads, `blastp` redoes the matches of a search's composition-based redo, when it has at least two, in jobs of up to four matches instead of one query per job, and replays the early termination test and the hit list insertions in the order of the one-thread redo, so reports do not change ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: blastp-fast human x yeast at 16 threads -32 % wall (`-subject`) and -20 % (`-db`) on the workstation, one thread unchanged, max RSS -5 MiB.
- `LOSAT_X_QDUSTPAR=1` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off): `blastn` (megablast, dc-megablast, blastn, and blastn-short with `-dust yes`) with two or more threads makes the DUST masks of a query batch on the search pool (the per-query map split over the pool's threads, collected in query order), instead of one query after the other before the lookup table is built; a query's mask depends on its letters and the DUST parameters only, so reports do not change (`LOSAT_X_QDUSTPARSHADOW=1` also makes the masks serially and compares them). On the workstation at 16 threads (D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, alternating pairs on 16 idle cores; every output has its expected sha256), the DUST step took 0.211 -> 0.019 s for 1,775 cDNAs (dc-megablast, one query batch), 0.286 -> 0.040 s for 3,492 cDNAs (dc-megablast, two batches) and 0.211 -> 0.022 s for blastn with the first input, and the whole search 2.19 -> 2.01 s, 2.95 -> 2.71 s and 1.83 -> 1.67 s (on/off 0.91-0.92); max RSS within 2 MiB for the first input (1-2 MiB higher with the switch on, within the run-to-run spread) and lower for the other two ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb -23..-27 % wall at 16 threads (dc-megablast; blastn of 31,168 cDNAs) on the workstation, max RSS unchanged.
- `LOSAT_X_QPREPPAR=1` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_QPREPPARSHADOW`): `blastn` (megablast, dc-megablast, blastn, blastn-short) with two or more threads encodes the queries of a batch (each into its own part of the batch's BLASTNA buffer) and computes the ungapped Karlin blocks of its contexts on the search pool, as ordered parallel maps, instead of one after the other before the lookup table is built; the encoding of a query and the block of a context are functions of their own letters and the scoring only, and the results are collected in query / context order, so reports do not change (`LOSAT_X_QPREPPARSHADOW=1` also computes both serially and compares the bytes and the float bits; its exit line is `[X_SHADOW] QPREPPAR batches= queries= contexts= fallbacks= diffs=0 identical`). On the workstation (D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISC`, `LOSAT_X_QBATCHPAR`, `LOSAT_X_DIAGLV`, `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARFOLD`, `LOSAT_X_QDUSTPAR` and `LOSAT_X_DISCLUTPAR` on in both arms; alternating pairs on 16 idle physical cores; every output has its expected sha256), the whole search took on/off 0.966 / 0.941 (1,775 cDNAs, dc-megablast, 4 / 16 threads), 0.963 / 0.922 (3,492 cDNAs) and 0.949 / 0.974 (31,168 cDNAs, 37 batches); the two steps took 0.100 -> 0.020 s for the second input at 16 threads and 0.55 -> 0.17 s for the third (summed over the batches); max RSS within 3 MiB ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: dc-megablast of D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb x0.92-0.97 at 4 and 16 threads on the workstation, max RSS within 3 MiB.
- Added the experimental switch `LOSAT_X_QSTREAM` (off unless set; not in the strict set; shadow `LOSAT_X_QSTREAMSHADOW`): `blastn` (every task, `-subject` and `-db`) with `LOSAT_X_RPTSTREAM` reads the query file twice instead of holding all its records for the whole search: a first reading keeps each record's length and how the reading ended (everything before the search, and every refusal or warning before the first output, comes from it as before), and a second handle on the same file reads each batch's records when the batch loop or a `LOSAT_X_QBATCHPAR` slot needs them, as NCBI reads its query batches one at a time; reports, `-out` files and standard error do not change (`LOSAT_X_QSTREAMSHADOW=1` also reads the file the old way and compares every batch's records). Standard input, pipes and devices are read once as before. On the workstation (31,168 D. melanogaster cDNAs, 96 MB, against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, with the blastn candidate switches and `LOSAT_X_RPTSTREAM`, on vs off in one window; every output has its expected sha256), blastn's max RSS fell from 158 to 62 MiB at 1 thread (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db`: 126 MiB, another window), 289 -> 209 MiB at 4 and 837 -> 755 MiB at 16 threads, dc-megablast's from 180 to 90 MiB at 1 thread (NCBI 150) and 488 -> 413 MiB at 16, with the wall within 0.99-1.03 (one round each at 1 thread); at 16 threads on query files of 5-40 MB it saved no memory and the extra parse made the search 1-4 % slower (1.018 on 5.4 MB in 8 rounds, 1.035 on 40 MB, noisy), so it stays opt-in ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- Added the switch `LOSAT_X_BPREDOLPT` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; it acts only with `LOSAT_X_BPREDOPAR`, whose shadow `LOSAT_X_BPREDOPARSHADOW` checks it): with two or more threads, each window of `blastp`'s parallel composition-based redo runs its jobs, when it has two or more, in one scope of at most one task per thread (`min(threads, jobs)`), and the tasks take the jobs one at a time, largest first (query letters times subject letters), instead of a parallel iterator that splits them in halves, so that fewer threads wait at the end of a window; the results are read by job index as before, so reports do not change. On the workstation at 16 threads, blastp-fast of 23,879 human proteins against the yeast proteome (`-subject`, with `LOSAT_X_BPREDOPAR` and `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJRUN`; alternating runs, the same outputs) took 7.84 -> 7.56 s (x0.964) with query blocks of 50,000 letters and 10.60 -> 10.17 s (x0.960) with 20,000; the redo windows took 0.28 and 0.42 s less, and peak memory was equal or lower ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17 (it acts only with `LOSAT_X_BPREDOPAR`): blastp-fast human x yeast `-subject` at 16 threads -9 % wall on the workstation.
- `LOSAT_X_QBATCHPAR=1` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off): `blastn` with `-subject` and two or more threads searches NCBI's query batches side by side ahead of the batch loop, in slots of one or more threads, with a prediction of NCBI's adaptive batch sizes. The loop still reads every batch with NCBI's batch sizes and takes a slot's result only for that same batch, so reports and standard error do not change (`LOSAT_X_QBATCHPARSHADOW=1` compares every batch taken from a slot with the loop's own search; `LOSAT_X_STATS` prints a summary line). Every slot is stopped before the reports after the loop and anything an error path writes (with `LOSAT_X_RPTSTREAM`, on by default, each batch's reports are written when the loop takes the batch, while the slots search the batches ahead), and no batch planned after one that failed is started. The batches in flight follow a memory budget (`LOSAT_X_QBATCHPARMEM`; by default 96 MiB per thread beyond the first for blastn and 28 MiB for megablast and dc-megablast, where a 5 Mb dc-megablast batch is estimated at 79 MiB, so its budget allows a second slot from four threads, and a 5 Mb megablast batch, with its larger lookup table, at about 135 MiB, so from six threads). The slots come from a throughput model: the units of work are the planned batches of at least half the letters of the letter-weighted median batch (so one long record does not turn the slots off), and k slots of m threads are used only when the model (rounds of batches, contention between slots, and the speed of one batch on m threads, measured up to 16 threads and kept flat beyond) predicts them at least 3 % faster than the loop on all threads; `LOSAT_X_STATS` states the units, the layout judged and its prediction. This rule replaced the first one (units = planned letters over the largest batch, as many slots as the budget allowed) before any release. On the workstation (D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, 16 idle cores of one node, with `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISC`, `DIAGLV`, `PAIRPARFOLD`, `QDUSTPAR` and `DISCLUTPAR` in both arms, medians of 3 alternating rounds, every output with its expected sha256) the new rule took x0.78 of the first rule's time for one big dc-megablast batch (1,775 cDNAs) at 16 threads (the first rule left half of the threads idle), x0.80 and x0.69 for blastn with the same queries at 4 and 8 threads (it had one-thread slots), x0.91 for dc-megablast with 31,168 cDNAs at 4 threads (it had no slots), x0.93 for blastn with 31,168 cDNAs at 16 threads, and x0.96-1.00 in the other cells, where the layout stayed or the difference is within the rounds' spread; peak memory is higher where slots now run (dc-megablast 31,168 cDNAs at 4 threads 232 -> 312 MiB; NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 with `-db` took 432 MiB there in an earlier window; 3,492 cDNAs at 4 threads 111 -> 177 MiB) and where more batches are admitted in flight (dc-megablast 31,168 cDNAs at 16 threads 499 -> 518 MiB, blastn 31,168 cDNAs at 16 threads 1,079 -> 1,128 MiB), and lower or equal where they no longer run. Off with `-db`, and when the subjects already give every thread work (as many 5 Mb pieces as threads) ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17, with no default budget (so no slots) for a query file that NCBI's blastn itself would not split by queries (`-mt_mode 0` with `-db`: larger than 10,000,000 bytes, or 1,500,000 at a word size below 10, against fewer than 6e9 (1.5e8) letters of subjects, the `-subject` letters standing for the database; blastn_app.cpp `s_GetMT1Cutoffs`), where NCBI's max RSS does not grow with its threads: plain blastn of 1,775 cDNAs (5.4 MB) at 16 threads now keeps 122 MiB (332 with slots; NCBI `-db` 136); blastn of 31,168 cDNAs -42 % wall at 16 threads, max RSS below NCBI's (1,076 vs 1,897 MiB). The subjects' work units count the letters of `LOSAT_X_BNSTORE`'s packed subjects too.
- `blastp -task blastp-fast` builds each query block's compressed lookup table flat, in three passes, and with two or more threads on the search's thread pool (`LOSAT_X_BPCLUT`, one of the switches above; shadow `LOSAT_X_BPCLUTSHADOW`, opt-in), and schedules that build with one synchronization per pass, reuses the previous block's table memory instead of allocating and zeroing it again, and computes the query contexts' Karlin-Altschul parameters as tasks of the table build (`LOSAT_X_BPLKPFLAT`, one of the switches above; it acts only where `LOSAT_X_BPCLUT` builds the table on the pool; shadow `LOSAT_X_BPLKPFLATSHADOW`, opt-in). The table, and so the report, does not change. On the workstation (16 physical cores; medians of alternating rounds; every output hash equal) `blastp-fast` of the human proteome against the yeast proteome with `-subject` took, with both switches and `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJRUN` on against all three off, 0.784 of the time at 16 threads (blocks of 50,000 query letters; 0.741 with blocks of 20,000) and 0.849 at 8; `LOSAT_X_BPLKPFLAT` alone, with `LOSAT_X_BPCLUT` on in both runs, 0.893 (0.858) at 16 threads and 0.938 (0.928) at 8, with a peak memory of 102 against 103 MiB (75 against 85 MiB). Building the table on the pool instead of serially (measured on 2026-10-14 with earlier builds, 20,000-letter blocks) took 92.5 instead of 96.0 s at one thread and 26.2 instead of 55.4 s at 16. One thread and `blastp` (exact) build no table on the pool and are unchanged. `LOSAT_X_BPCLUT=0` and `LOSAT_X_BPLKPFLAT=0` turn them off ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- Added the switch `LOSAT_X_BPLKPAHEAD` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_BPLKPAHEADSHADOW`): where `LOSAT_X_BPLKPFLAT` acts in the query-block search of `blastp -task blastp-fast` with `LOSAT_X_BPCLUT` (not under `LOSAT_X_BPCLUTSHADOW`) (`-subject` or `-db`, two or more threads), the next block's query encoding and lookup table are built on the thread pool while the current block is searched, instead of at the start of the next block, with the same calls on the same queries, so the report does not change. One more block's table is held while it is built (21 MiB at 50,000-letter blocks, 13 MiB at 20,000), and its memory is allocated by the search's own thread; the build's temporary buffers now overlap the current block's search. Measured on the workstation at 16 threads (human x yeast proteomes, `-subject`, `LOSAT_X_BPLKPFLAT` and `LOSAT_X_BPSUBJRUN` on in both runs; 4 alternating pairs each): blocks of 50,000 letters 6.86 -> 6.48 s (x0.944), max RSS 102-104 -> 126-128 MiB; blocks of 20,000 letters 8.90 -> 8.14 s (x0.914), 75-77 -> 93-95 MiB (more than the second table alone; the rest is not attributed by measurement); at 8 threads x0.980 and x0.965; with `-db` and `LOSAT_X_BPDBCUT` 7.07 -> 6.60 s (x0.932), max RSS 93-96 -> 116-121 MiB (+34 MiB without `LOSAT_X_BPDBCUT`); the same output ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: blastp-fast human x yeast at 16 threads -6 % wall (`-subject`) and x0.932 (`-db` with `LOSAT_X_BPDBCUT`) on the workstation; max RSS +23-28 MiB, below NCBI's (123 vs 328 MiB, 116 vs 582).
- `blastp` (and `blastp -task blastp-fast`) runs its OrthoFinder-work paths by default (`LOSAT_X_BPSEED`, `LOSAT_X_BPUNGAP`, `LOSAT_X_BPGAP`, `LOSAT_X_BPLUT`, `LOSAT_X_BPPFREE`, `LOSAT_X_BPPCOMPACT`, `LOSAT_X_BPREDOFIT` and `LOSAT_X_BPQBLOCK`, among the switches above; `LOSAT_X_BPUNGAP` also reaches `blastx`). Reports do not change. A `-outfmt 6` `blastp` search (`-subject` or `-db`) of more than 50,000 query letters in more than one query batch now runs in query blocks of whole batches (`LOSAT_X_BPQBLOCK`, 50,000 letters by default; `LOSAT_X_BPQBLOCK=0` searches all queries together), with the same rows and a lower peak memory; with `-db` only when no definition line of the database can fail a batch (#190). In a 49-job run of 7-species protein searches with all eight on, every output equalled NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0's `-db` output (or the known `-subject` / `-db` difference) and the max RSS was below NCBI's on every job.
- `LOSAT_X_DISCLUTPAR` (the entries below) is on by default since 2026-10-17 (in the strict set; `LOSAT_X_DISCLUTPAR=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turn it off), and in a table's first chunk its fill writes 0 into the cells' pages before the update reads them, where they form whole 2 MiB regions of the table that the chunk writes in full, so the update finds those pages mapped instead of mapping the kernel's zero page and copying it (two faults, and a TLB shootdown to the search's other CPUs). The zeros go over zeros and only where the fill writes every page anyway, so tables and reports do not change, nor max RSS with 4 KiB pages or with transparent huge pages (a first version that touched every page the chunk wrote raised max RSS under THP `always` for small queries, up to the whole table; with whole regions only, max RSS on such a host equalled the fill's without the round at every size measured, 1 kb to 1.3 Mb). It runs only when the first chunk has at least 8 positions per page of the table. On the workstation (Linux, THP `madvise`; D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, dc-megablast, the other blastn switches of the line on in every arm, 16 idle physical cores, rotated rounds, every output with its expected sha256), with the first version of the round, the table fill took, switch off / the switch before / with the round: 1,775 cDNAs 0.140 / 0.089 / 0.080 s at 2 threads, 0.170 / 0.081 / 0.071 at 4, 0.204 / 0.051 / 0.043 at 16; 3,492 cDNAs 0.264 / 0.151 / 0.149, 0.287 / 0.168 / 0.152, 0.375 / 0.091 / 0.080; 31,168 cDNAs (37 batches) 2.19 / 1.21 / 1.24 at 4 and 2.72 / 1.13 / 1.11 at 16 (the 1.24 came from the readers' marking loop, now run only in a table's first chunk; the final form, measured on 2026-10-18 in the same way, took 1.20 s at 4 threads and 1.08 s at 16 against 1.22 / 1.15 s for the switch before, 0.91-1.00 of that switch's fill for 1,775 and 3,492 cDNAs at 2-16 threads, with 68,000-112,000 TLB shootdowns per run at 16 threads against its 108,000-261,000, since whole regions leave partly written ones to the fill's own faults); the whole search took 0.68-0.95 of the switch-off time at 2-16 threads (1.00 at 1 thread, where the loop runs either way), the TLB shootdowns per run at 16 threads fell from 108,000-301,000 to 100-5,300, and max RSS stayed within the runs' spread and below NCBI BLAST+ `-db` ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `LOSAT_X_DISCLUTPAR` (the next entry) fills its tables without false sharing: each bucket of the fill keeps its header on a cache line of its own, and each thread counts the chains of its cells (the counters of the longest chain) in a private copy, added into the table's counters once after the fill, so the threads no longer write one another's counter or bucket-header lines (the presence-vector words of two neighbouring blocks still share a line; that was measured to cost nothing). Tables and reports do not change. The cause, measured with hardware counters on the workstation: the 4-byte counters of 16 threads (4 at 4 threads) shared each 64-byte line, and so did the bucket headers of neighbouring threads, 3.2-4.5 million HITM loads and as many RFOs (line transfers between cores) per fill of the 1,775 cDNAs (6.4-9.0 million of each for the 3,492), against at most a few thousand in the loop; the jobs' summed time of #252's fill (3.8 and 4.9 times the loop's) was stall time, its instructions were 1.46-1.6 times the loop's. On the workstation (the inputs of the next entry, 16 idle physical cores of one node, rotated rounds of the switch off, the switch of #252 and this one; every output has its expected sha256) the table fill took, off / #252 / now: 1,775 cDNAs 0.141 / 0.196 / 0.090 s at 2 threads, 0.171 / 0.197 / 0.083 s at 4, 0.203 / 0.127 / 0.060 s at 8 and 0.201 / 0.082 / 0.049 s at 16; 3,492 cDNAs 0.259 / 0.366 / 0.155, 0.368 / 0.375 / 0.151, 0.374 / 0.231 / 0.100 and 0.310 / 0.154 / 0.083 s (medians over all runs; the loop's fill depends on the machine's speed mode, for 1,775 cDNAs at 4 threads 0.139 s in the fast mode and 0.204 s in the slow one, and the pool fills much less; within one mode this switch's fill is 0.22-0.64 of the loop's and 0.39-0.59 of #252's). So the switch now beats the loop from 2 threads on in every mode and needs no thread threshold. Whole search, runs in the same speed mode of the machine: 0.92-0.98 of #252's and 0.93-0.96 of the loop's wall (1,775 cDNAs at 16 threads 2.190 / 2.062 / 2.027 s; 3,492 cDNAs at 4 threads 4.030 / 4.045 / 3.854 s); max RSS medians at most 2.5 MiB above the loop's and within 1 MiB of #252's. The private counters take threads x 8 KiB per template at word size 11 (x 32 KiB at 12) and the bucket headers 64 bytes each ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `LOSAT_X_DISCLUTPAR=1` (off unless set; not in the strict set; shadow `LOSAT_X_DISCLUTPARSHADOW`): `blastn -task dc-megablast` (and megablast given `-template_type`, `-template_length` and a word size of 11 or 12), with `-subject` or `-db` and two or more threads, fills its discontiguous lookup tables (one or two templates) on the search pool instead of in one loop: each thread reads the words of a part of the queries, each word goes to the thread that owns its table cell, which applies the words of its cells in the original order, and each thread then links the words it read, so each cell gets its words in the same order and reports do not change. On the workstation at 16 threads (D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L with `LOSAT_X_PAIRPARDISC=1`, alternating pairs on 16 idle cores; every output has its expected sha256), the table build took 0.206 -> 0.089 s for 1,775 cDNAs (one query batch) and 0.378 -> 0.158 s for 3,492 cDNAs (two batches), the whole search 2.17 -> 2.05 s and 4.64 -> 4.43 s (on/off 0.94 and 0.96); at 4 threads the search did not change (on/off 1.00; the build 0.21 s either way, and 0.06 s longer with the switch in the machine's fast phase). With `LOSAT_TIMING` a second line, `[TIMING] disc_lookup_split`, splits the build into the allocation, the loop or the pool's phases (wall, longest job, summed job time, latest start) and the longest chain: on the workstation the joins cost about 0.2 ms each (0.01 s per search), and the time is in the phases' own run time, whose sum is 3.8 times the loop's fill at 4 threads and 4.9 times at 16 (the cause, false sharing, was measured afterwards and is fixed: see the entry above). The buckets of the fill reserve about 8 MiB per template on typical input and at most 24 MiB plus 544 bytes x threads^2 per template whatever the input (before, unmasked short repeats grew them to up to 2 x threads x 4 MiB); max RSS within about 4 MiB of the run without the switch ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `LOSAT_X_RPTSTREAM=1` (in the strict set, perf/perfmb-default-bundle; `=0` or `LOSAT_X_DEFAULT=0` turns it off; shadow `LOSAT_X_RPTSTREAMSHADOW`): `blastn` (every task, `-subject` and `-db`) writes the reports of each query batch as soon as its batch loop takes the batch, as NCBI prints each batch's results in its loop, and drops the batch's final hit lists, instead of keeping the lists of every batch until one write after the loop; reports, `-out` files and standard error do not change (except the order of `LOSAT_TIMING`'s blocks: a query's report depends only on its own hit list, and outfmt 7's last line counts the queries of every batch; `LOSAT_X_RPTSTREAMSHADOW=1` also writes the reports the old way in memory and compares the bytes). With two or more batches it does not act where one of LOSAT's own refusals could come after the first batch (the reports of earlier batches would then come before it, where today none does); with `-db` that means one pass over the database's definition lines and ambiguity data before the search (0.02 s for 28,036 16S sequences and 0.11 s for 465,769 human cDNAs at 16 threads on the workstation, 0.3 % and 0.8 % of the search). It does not act either for the Web adapter's hit records and HSP observer, for file sinks, for two or more formats, or with debug output. On the workstation (31,168 D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, `-outfmt 6`, with the six blastn candidate switches, alternating rounds; every output has its expected sha256), blastn's max RSS fell from 502 to 157 MiB at 1 thread (NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0 `-db`: 126 MiB, another window), 638 -> 295 MiB at 4 and 1,076 -> 775 MiB at 16 threads, and the 16-thread search took 7.37 -> 6.13 s (on/off 0.83) because the report write now overlaps the search of the batches ahead; dc-megablast, a smaller input and two `-db` searches changed by 0.96-1.03 (the 1.03 of the smaller dc-megablast input was 1.00 in 8 more rounds). `LOSAT::algorithm::blastn::hsp::write_output_blastn_hitlists_to_writer` now takes the epilog's query count (`Option<usize>`) instead of a flag ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)). On by default since 2026-10-17: blastn of 31,168 cDNAs against 3 Mb on the workstation, max RSS 502 -> 157 MiB at one thread, 1,076 -> 775 MiB and x0.831 wall at 16 threads.
- Added the experimental switch `LOSAT_X_MBLUTPAD` (off unless set; not in the strict set): where `LOSAT_X_PAIRPAR` (in the strict set) fills the contiguous megablast lookup table on the search pool (`blastn` megablast and blastn with two or more threads, `-subject` and `-db`), each thread counts the table's longest-chain counters and sets its presence bits in private copies on 64-byte lines of their own and adds them into the table for its own cells at the end, instead of in the shared arrays, whose lines held the counters of up to 16 threads (false sharing); the counters and bits are the same, so reports do not change (`LOSAT_X_PAIRPARSHADOW=1` with the switch builds the table again that way and compares every array; `[X_SHADOW] MBLUTPAD` at exit). On the workstation (D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, alternating off/on rounds on 16 idle cores; every output has its expected sha256), for megablast of 31,168 cDNAs (37 tables) the table fill took 1.49 -> 1.29 s at 16 threads, 1.66 -> 1.35 s at 8, 2.26 -> 1.88 s at 4 and 3.03 -> 2.85 s at 2, and the search 0.93-0.97 of its time (per round); for megablast and blastn of 1,775 cDNAs and blastn of 31,168 the fill took 0.85-0.97 of its time and the searches did not change beyond their run-to-run spread. At 64 threads (32 cores and their siblings) it loses where a search fills only a few tables: megablast of the E. coli genomes took 1.30-1.52 times as long (the fill 1.66-2.01 times), while the 31,168 cDNAs tied (search 0.96, fill 1.04), so the switch is for 2-16 threads; a buffer freed after each fill instead was no better (fill 1.01-1.08 of the kept buffer's at 16 threads, max RSS -3.4 to +0.5 MiB). Max RSS rises by up to the threads' copies where a search's peak includes them (on Linux with glibc each fill keeps its threads' copies in one buffer that the next fill reuses and never grows, mapped at 32 MiB + 4 KiB or more of address space of which only the written part is resident; elsewhere the buffer is freed after each fill): +2 to +7 MiB at 16 threads on inputs of 1,775 cDNAs and of E. coli genomes, never above NCBI's, which keeps the switch off by default for now. With `LOSAT_TIMING`, `[TIMING] x_mb_lookup_build` and `[TIMING] x_mb_lookup_split` give the build time of the megablast tables split into allocation, loop or pool fill and longest chain ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- Added the experimental switch `LOSAT_X_MBLUTONCE` (off unless set; not in the strict set: it wins time on megablast and costs max RSS, see the end of this entry): where `LOSAT_X_PAIRPAR` fills the contiguous megablast lookup table on the search pool (`blastn` megablast and blastn with two or more threads, `-subject` and `-db`), each query position is read once instead of by every thread: per chunk of 2^19 positions each thread reads a span and hands each word to the thread that owns its table cell, which applies the words of its cells in reading order (into the private copies of `LOSAT_X_MBLUTPAD`), and the reader then links its words; blastn's tables take the words in reverse order, the order of the loop's re-threading, without its per-thread word lists. Each cell gets the same words in the same order, so the table and reports do not change (`LOSAT_X_PAIRPARSHADOW=1` builds the table again that way and compares every array; `[X_SHADOW] MBLUTONCE` at exit; in the gates of the pull request 4,623 tables of 3,227 runs, 1,258,137,866 elements, all equal). One arena per concurrent fill of at most 8.3 MiB whatever the thread count (8.0 MiB at 16 and 64 threads; less for small queries; with `LOSAT_X_QBATCHPAR` each slot that fills a table at the same time holds its own, about k x 8 MiB for k slots, which its batch memory estimate does not count), freed at the end of the fill (on Linux with glibc a mapping of at least 32 MiB + 4 KiB that is unmapped). With `LOSAT_TIMING`, `once=` in `[TIMING] x_mb_lookup_build` and a `[TIMING] x_mb_lookup_once` line split the fill into its phases. On the workstation (2026-10-18; D. melanogaster cDNAs against 3 Mb of chromosome 2L, rotated rounds) megablast of 31,168 cDNAs took 0.82-0.85 of the search time at 2-64 threads (the fill 0.39-0.64), 0.84 / 0.89 with 4 / 8 `LOSAT_X_QBATCHPAR` slots at 64 threads, and megablast of 1,775 cDNAs 0.88; blastn and a megablast of three E. coli genomes did not change beyond their spread; max RSS rose by up to 10 MiB at 2-16 threads and 31 MiB at 64 (blastn of 1,775 cDNAs at 16 threads 145 MiB against NCBI's 144), so it stays off by default ([`docs/agents/diagnostics.md`](docs/agents/diagnostics.md)).
- `blastp`'s two-hit seed stage tests the word hits of one lookup cell eight at a time in AVX2 registers by default where the CPU has AVX2 (`LOSAT_X_BPDIAG`, one of the switches above; other CPUs keep the per-hit test). Reports do not change. In a second run of the same 49 jobs with it on as well, every output equalled the run without it, and the sum of the job times was 0.457 of NCBI BLAST+ 2.17.0's (0.597 without it; NCBI's times from an earlier run of the same jobs).

### WebAssembly and LOSAT Web

- LOSAT Web: the five mode tabs fit a 320 px window, so the search page no longer scrolls sideways and every mode name
  stays inside its tab (#1329).
- LOSAT Web: error messages are easier to read on the grey page and in a selected row; the error colour is a little
  darker and now meets 4.5:1 on every ground it is drawn on (#1330).
- LOSAT Web: an input that holds no sequence (an empty file, whitespace only, or a defline only) no longer gets the
  check mark and "Accepted"; its card says that the input adds nothing to the search (#1337).
- LOSAT Web: Back on the results returns to the Search tab with the form as it was, and Forward shows the results
  again, instead of leaving LOSAT and losing every run and input (#1339).
- LOSAT Web: when the browser does not let the page store result files, the note under the form says in LOSAT's words
  that results are kept in this tab's memory (at most 512 MB) and why, instead of printing the browser's error text;
  in Firefox with the site's data blocked, the page now starts (#1340).
- LOSAT Web: a number and its unit, such as "512 MB", "420 ms" or "40 nt", stay on one line (#1341).
- LOSAT Web: when a part of LOSAT cannot be loaded or stops, the page says so once, with what to do. It asks again by
  itself once the part can be loaded, keeping the pasted inputs, and no run or button hangs; Cancel also works while a
  run's results are being organized (#1343).
- LOSAT Web: a search runs to its results in Firefox with the site's data blocked, instead of failing with "The
  operation is insecure" (#1346).
- LOSAT Web: the header reads "LOSAT" with "A miniaturized reimplementation of NCBI BLAST algorithm"; the "?" help of
  an option shows while the "?" is pointed at or reached with the keyboard, and a click keeps it open, as NCBI's
  helpbox; the screen wake lock is on by default, offered as "Keep searching while you're away" on phones and
  tablets, whose screens turn off by themselves and end the search.
- README: on WASI with Node.js, a path such as `gone/../db` opens `db` even when `gone` does not exist (Node.js reads `..` as text); NCBI BLAST+ and native LOSAT on Linux stop with an error.
- WASI command builds (`wasm32-wasip1`, `wasm32-wasip1-threads`): makeblastdb's log time
  (`Building a new DB, current time:`) and the database's date (blastdbcmd `-info`'s `Date:`, a
  report's `Posted date:`) are the local time, as natively, where they were UTC: the command reads
  `TZ`, `TZDIR` and `/etc/localtime` as glibc does, from the environment and the directories its
  host gives it. `New DB name:` and `Deleted existing ... named` print the absolute path, as
  natively, where they printed the `-out` name as given: the name is made absolute with the working
  directory that the command takes from the host's `PWD`. Native builds give the same bytes as
  before, except for a `-out` name or a working directory with C spaces (a tab, a newline) at its
  ends, which makeblastdb now cuts as NCBI's `ConcatPath` does. The WASI command checks
  (`LOSAT/tests/check_wasm_threading.py`) run makeblastdb, blastdbcmd `-info` and a blastn report in
  a working directory with `TZ` a zone file, a POSIX rule and unset through each WASI command.
- WASI command builds (`wasm32-wasip1`, `wasm32-wasip1-threads`): `makeblastdb -logfile FILE` now writes
  the log and the messages to the file as natively. Before, every such run stopped with
  `Command line argument error: Argument "logfile". File is not accessible` (exit status 1): makeblastdb
  wrote its log through a second handle of the file that its messages go to, which WASI cannot make. It now
  writes both through the one open file, in the order they are written; native builds give the same bytes
  as before. The WASI command checks (`LOSAT/tests/check_wasm_threading.py`) run every makeblastdb
  `-logfile` row of the drop-in corpus (`dropin_corpus.py check`) and two runs appending a log and a
  warning to one file through each WASI command.
- WASI command builds (`wasm32-wasip1`, `wasm32-wasip1-threads`): blastx with `-outfmt` 5 (BLAST XML) and
  13 to 16 (XML2 and JSON) now runs as natively. Before, every such run stopped with
  `Command line argument error: Argument "query". File is not accessible` (exit status 1): in these formats
  LOSAT reads the query file once before the search, and it read a second handle on the file, which WASI
  cannot make. It now reads the open file itself and puts it back where it was; native builds give the same
  bytes as before. The WASI command checks (`LOSAT/tests/check_wasm_threading.py`) run every blastx row of
  these formats of the frozen `-subject` and `-db` fixtures through each WASI command (`--outfmts` of
  `check_losat.py` and `db_search_fixtures.py check`).
- LOSAT Web and the WebAssembly (wasm32) builds now do what the command line does where they differed:
  a BLASTX batch whose lookup table would need over 268 million entries is built by the reference
  builder (same output) instead of stopping with "capacity overflow"; rpsbproc prints NCBI's text
  for a `cdtrack.txt` line that ends early (`__pos (which is 18446744073709551615)`, not
  `4294967295`); a `-window_masker_taxid` run without a counts file reports NCBI's "could not open "
  where WASI used to open its root directory; and on WASI hosts `-export_search_strategy X -out X`
  and `-out_pssm X -out X` are refused as on Linux (a symbolic or hard link to the `-out` file is
  not seen there, because Rust's standard library has no stable file identity on WASI).
- WASI command builds (`wasm32-wasip1`, `wasm32-wasip1-threads`): every search program (blastn,
  blastp, blastx, tblastn, tblastx, psiblast, rpsblast, rpstblastn) now reads standard input as
  natively: `-query -`, a run without `-query` (its default is `-`), and `-subject -` where the
  program reads it. Before, each such run stopped with "reading the query from standard input ('-')
  on this platform is not supported by LOSAT's <PROGRAM>" (exit status 1). LOSAT now reads the
  process's standard input in place, as NCBI reads `cin`, instead of a copy of its descriptor, which
  WASI cannot make; native builds give the same bytes as before. The WASI command checks
  (`LOSAT/tests/check_wasm_threading.py`) compare standard-input runs of every search program with
  the native command.
- WASI command builds (`wasm32-wasip1`, `wasm32-wasip1-threads`): psiblast with a PSSM as the query
  (`-in_pssm FILE`) now runs, as natively, instead of stopping with "reading the query from standard
  input ('-') on this platform is not supported by LOSAT's PSIBLAST" (NCBI reads no query then; LOSAT
  had opened the default `-query -`, whose standard input WASI cannot copy), and its own errors come in
  NCBI's order; `-out_pssm` (the ASN.1 PSSM file) is written instead of the command panicking
  (`std::process::id()`, which WASI does not have, named the staging file). The serial and threaded
  command checks (`LOSAT/tests/check_wasm_threading.py`) now run every psiblast row of
  `outfmt0_manifest.tsv` through each WASI command.
- The Node serial runner (`run_losat_wasi.js`, shipped with the Wasm commands) waits for a full standard output or error pipe instead of failing, as the threaded runner does. The warning that Node prints when the runner loads WASI made a piped standard error non-blocking, and standard output too when both share one pipe (`2>&1`), so more than about 64 KiB of report or warnings to a slow reader stopped with `Error: Resource temporarily unavailable (os error 6)` and exit 1 (a BLASTP report of 280,491 bytes stopped after 63,264 bytes of output). The runner now repeats such a write every 1 ms until the pipe takes it, as the native command's write blocks. The cargo test runner for `wasm32-wasip1` (`web/tools/wasi-test-runner.mjs`) and the Wasm benchmark runners wait the same way. Reports do not change.
- LOSAT Web writes the formats that a run's argv asks for where another format of the run fails in the middle of its report, as the command line writes them: with `-sorthits` and a description table whose last total is above 99999 bits (where NCBI BLAST+ pads outfmt 0 without end), `-outfmt 6`, a field list and 5 are written and outfmt 0 is left empty with LOSAT's refusal in the warnings; so for an NCBI error of outfmt 0 alone, such as BLASTX's `Row is empty`; and in BLASTX, a tabular format that the argv does not ask for and that a later query batch refuses no longer fails the run (all three failed the whole run in LOSAT Web before). The engine module tells the page to discard what it received of such a format (ABI v2 version 3, stream 9; line RG, T7-W). In a run of two searches (`-num_descriptions` or `-num_alignments` giving outfmt 0 a hit list size of its own), the warnings show each message once: a query's warning no longer comes again before the error of outfmt 0.
- LOSAT Web's page uses `-conffile`, `-logfile` and `-dryrun` typed in Additional options: it asks for the file that
  `-conffile` names and the run reads it under that name; the `-logfile` file is shown after the output formats, saved
  with Download All, and kept under a failed run's status (TBLASTX writes its run messages and NCBI's error there); a
  dry run says that it searched nothing. Before, every `-conffile` run failed with NCBI's `Registry file "NAME" not
  found`, the `-logfile` file was dropped, and a dry run looked like a search with no hits.
- LOSAT Web's engine runs the NCBI C++ Toolkit options `-logfile`, `-conffile` and `-dryrun` in blastn, blastp, blastx,
  tblastn and tblastx as the command line does. They were refused. The file of `-conffile` is a file that the page gives
  the engine, the `-logfile` file comes back with the run's outputs, and a dry run checks its arguments and writes no
  output.
- LOSAT Web: BLASTN's `-window_masker_taxid`. The "Species-specific repeats filter for:" row gains a
  "Taxonomy ID" box beside the "Counts file" choice; "Download" fetches that taxonomy ID's
  `wmasker.obinary` from ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov only when pressed, keeps it in the browser (a stopped
  download continues with "Continue"), and searches read it from there with the same output as the
  command line. The run details show the taxonomy ID, the file's date and its SHA-256.
  The engine looks for the counts file of a taxonomy ID through the same counts source as
  `-window_masker_db` (the file system for the command line, unchanged; the registered files for the
  browser, under `windowmasker/<taxid>/wmasker.obinary`); ABI v2 stays version 2.
- Web: in WebKit (the engine of Safari; measured in Playwright's WebKit on Linux), cancelling a
  search that runs on several threads no longer uses up the page's shared memory. A cancelled
  search's thread that waited without a timeout stayed alive in WebKit, and with it the 4 GiB memory
  reservation of its search; a page holds about 15, so after some cancels a search of several threads
  ran on one thread ("could not reserve ... shared memory"). The shared-memory guard of the threaded
  module (`LOSAT/tests/wasi_shared_memory.js`, version 4) now has each wait record its address in a
  table of 128 cells while it waits, and the keeper of the memory ends those waits before a cancelled
  runtime ends: they then wait in slices of at most 100 ms with a call of the module's `sched_yield`
  import between them, where the browser ends the thread. Waits keep their own timeout until then, so
  searches do not slow down. The Engine worker's own wait for a thread to start waits in the same
  slices. In Playwright's WebKit, 20 cancels in one page kept every search at 4 threads and freed every
  memory; before, 10 to 15 memories stayed after 12 to 16 cancels in 3 of 4 pages, and one page ran
  its 16th search on one thread. Outputs do not change; Chromium and Firefox ended such threads
  already.
- LOSAT Web: BLASTN's `-window_masker_db` (WindowMasker). "Filters and Masking" gains NCBI's
  "Species-specific repeats" mark with a file field for the WindowMasker counts (NCBI's
  `wmasker.obinary` or a `windowmasker -mk_counts` obinary / binary file); the search masks the
  queries as the command line does, with the same output and the same errors. The counts are read
  once and kept for the next BLASTN searches of the session; the run details show the file's name
  and SHA-256. ABI v2 gains four exports for named input files (`file_begin`, `file_chunk`,
  `file_end`, `file_release`; still version 2).
- LOSAT Web refuses `-window_masker_taxid` other than 0 (it has no directory of counts files yet).
- LOSAT Web: a `.gz` or `.zst` source is refused exactly as the command line refuses it, with the
